# ADVANCED CRASH REPORTER - ASSERT OR BREAKPOINT (unhandled)
================================================================================
Game version   : 2.5 (3.0.88.46235, built 2026-10-19)
Report version : 1.5.0
Generated     : 2026-10-30 01:20:27.953
Process uptime : 3m 58.738s
Process id     : 408560
Faulting thread: 409372  (WATCHDOG DETECTOR)
Main thread    : 407064
Analyzed thread: 400056  redDispatcher15  (LIKELY BLOCKING WORKER)
Summary        : The game froze for 2 minutes while loading a save and closed itself. No mod's code was running in the part of the game that was stuck.
Verdict        : Unknown culprit
Technical      : redDispatcher15 held up the game loop in Cyberpunk2077.exe [GAME-EXE]; no mod code executing on the analyzed thread; objects: gameEffectorSystem, PlayerPuppet player_wa_fpp_ep1.ent, worldRuntimeEntityRegistry

================================================================================
# WHAT HAPPENED
================================================================================
The game froze for 2 minutes while loading a save and closed itself. No mod's
code was running in the part of the game that was stuck.

Unknown culprit

What to try:
  1. Load the save again: a load that freezes once often goes through the next
     time.
  2. If this save freezes every time, disable the mods you installed or updated
     most recently, or load an older save.
  3. Tools that track every game object (RedHotTools) can slow the game down
     until it freezes: disable them when you are not making mods.

-- Technical details -----------------------------------------------------------
Exception code : 0x80000003  (EXCEPTION_BREAKPOINT)
Chance         : unhandled
Faulting code  : 0x00007FF7F05D8E16  Cyberpunk2077.exe            +0x2FB8E16  (CWatchdogThread::OnCheckStatus+0xA6)  [GAME-EXE]

The engine's WatchdogThread raised this breakpoint after the game stopped making
progress. WatchdogThread detected the timeout; it is not the code that was stuck.

ACR selected thread 400056 (redDispatcher15) as the diagnostic focus because it was the sole non-parked redDispatcher worker.
Non-parked redDispatcher workers: 400056. Parked dispatchers waiting for work were excluded.
The GameThread was waiting for jobs to finish (job::prv::Dispatcher::FlushCounter):
the work holding it up ran on the job threads.

================================================================================
# ENGINE OBJECTS
================================================================================
Game objects this thread was working on: the ones its registers and the top of its
stack point to, named by the engine's own type information. Registers come first,
then the stack from the innermost frame out, so the first lines are the likeliest
to be what the code was handling. A component gives its name and the appearance it
came from, an entity its .ent and its appearance; a file a mod ships, its archive.
A TweakDB record, or a bare id of one (TweakDBID), gives its name and the mod
files that change it. A widget of the HUD or a menu gives its name, the mod scripts
that give it that name, and the widgets it sits in ("in a < b", innermost first).
